A reasoning LLM is the most accurate, at about 70x the cost of a fast LLM. In closed loops, large open LLMs succeed most often (up to 32.9%); small System-1 models rarely succeed.

Title: From One Query to Many: A Critical Survey of Privacy Accounting in RAG

  • Authors: Zhiyuan Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21245
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21245
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21245]] From One Query to Many: A Critical Survey of Privacy Accounting in RAG(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21245)
  • Keywords: privacy, protect, large language model
  • Abstract: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enables large language models to leverage external knowledge but introduces privacy risks when the retrieved corpus contains sensitive information. Differential privacy (DP) offers formal protection, yet interpreting and comparing privacy guarantees across existing DP-RAG methods remains challenging. In particular, a privacy budget reported for a single response does not directly characterize privacy loss over repeated queries, and even identical values of $\varepsilon$ may represent different guarantees depending on the protected data unit, accounting horizon, and observable outputs. This survey critically examines how privacy accounting evolves from single-query generation to multi-query RAG systems. We categorize existing approaches into query-time privatization, multi-query accounting, and one-time corpus privatization. We analyze their underlying assumptions and privacy--utility trade-offs. Rather than ranking methods by $\varepsilon$ alone, we propose a framework that interprets a DP-RAG guarantee along three dimensions: privacy granularity, accounting scope, and release boundaries. We conclude that an $\eps$ is a claim only together with the three slots: the protected unit, the accounting scope, and the release boundary.

Title: Fractional Power-of-Two Quantization for Efficient and Effective Multiplier-Free LLM Inference

  • Authors: Sunghyun Wee, Geunjae Choi, Hyeonjin Kim, Suyoung Kim, Nojun Kwak
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21257
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21257
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21257]] Fractional Power-of-Two Quantization for Efficient and Effective Multiplier-Free LLM Inference(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21257)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) demand efficient inference, where the multiply-accumulate (MAC) operations in linear projections dominate compute and energy cost. Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces this cost by mapping weights and activations to low-bit integers. Power-of-Two (PoT) weight quantization promises efficient multiplier-free LLM inference via shift-add logic, but suffers significant accuracy degradation at low bitwidths due to its coarse base-2 quantization grid. We introduce Fractional PoT (FPoT), a base-$\sqrt{2}$ (half-octave) fractional PoT grid that achieves practical 4-bit weight quantization while preserving a fully integer shift-add datapath. The method combines (i) the base-$\sqrt{2}$ weight grid with uniform integer activations, (ii) a calibration-time grid-alignment refinement absorbed into the static per-channel scale, and (iii) a lightweight 1-adder $\sqrt{2}$ approximation whose error is absorbed during weight calibration via approximate-grid quantization, yielding a processing element (PE) with significantly fewer gates than a 4-bit integer multiplier. On Llama-3-8B with 4-bit weight and activation quantization, FPoT improves zero-shot commonsense reasoning performance by 8.72% relative to the PoT baseline, while maintaining performance close to the uniform integer baseline, with only a negligible 0.4%p degradation. These results hold across various configurations spanning seven models and three bit-widths. Furthermore, FPoT can be integrated with state-of-the-art LLM PTQ methods, demonstrating framework-agnostic applicability.

Title: Can Capability Enhancement be Localized?

  • Authors: Anh Tuan Nguyen, Anna Bair, Zico Kolter
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21294
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21294

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Transformer-based language models have shown strong capabilities across many tasks and domains. However, prior work shows that, for a given task, not all attention heads matter equally. Specifically, they suggest that many \textit{redundant} heads can be safely removed with little-to-no effect, while a small set of \textit{important} heads, when ablated, severely damages the model's performance on the given task. In this work, we investigate a complementary aspect, called \textit{Capability Enhancement Localization} (CEL): \textit{given a model and a task, does there exist a small set of heads whose removal improves the task performance?} We first investigate this question \textit{empirically} across models from different families. Using a simple two-stage routine that screens candidate heads and evaluates their joint ablations, we identify sparse head combinations whose removal improves task-specific performance without further fine-tuning. Motivated by these observations, we ask \textit{theoretically whether such a phenomenon can naturally arise from training dynamics, and under what conditions}. To this end, we construct a simplified multi-head softmax attention model and two tasks with conflicting features, and show that gradient descent from small random initialization provably concentrates the conflicting features into particular heads. We further show that ablating such heads improves performance on one task while harming the other, even though training converges to a global optimum of the joint objective. This establishes a possible training-induced theoretical explanation for CEL.

Title: Personalized Projected Ascent for Federated Stackelberg Optimization

  • Authors: M Yashwanth, Sharannya Ghosh, Saikiran Bulusu, Anirban Chakraborty
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21295
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21295
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21295]] Personalized Projected Ascent for Federated Stackelberg Optimization(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21295)
  • Keywords: robust, federate
  • Abstract: Federated minimax problems, such as distributionally robust optimization and adversarial training, include an inner maximization variable built from each client's local data. These variables differ in meaning and dimension from one client to the next, so averaging them across clients, as standard federated minimax methods do, is ill-posed. We therefore study a federated minimax problem where the minimization variable $\mathbf{x}$ is shared and aggregated, while each client retains the maximization variable $\mathbf{y}_i$ (personalized). Because these applications constrain $\mathbf{y}_i$ to a compact convex set, we analyze projected ascent directly. We show that, in the nonconvex-concave regime, this projected personalized scheme attains the standard sublinear rate. In the nonconvex-PL regime, however, it hits a stepsize-invariant noise floor due to stochastic $\mathbf{y}_i$ updates. To mitigate this, we propose VR-SGDA, incorporating a variance-reduced ascent step where each client maintains a private variable snapshot. By evaluating current and snapshot gradients on the same sample, the constant variance is replaced by a vanishing drift under a two-timescale regime. VR-SGDA eliminates the noise floor, attaining sublinear leading rates while preserving personalization and projection. Experiments on subpopulation-robustness and NLP adversarial training confirm both the worst-group robustness benefits and the two-timescale tracking mechanism.

Title: Spectrum Is Not Enough: A Random-Matrix Account of Data-Free Preconditioning for Differentially Private Learning

  • Authors: Shilin Zhang, Kangning Wang

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21312

  • Keywords: privacy, data-free

A single dichotomy organizes it: in the factorization $ A \otimes G $, the error-signal factor $ G $ and the architecture-determined bulk of the activation factor $ A $ are reconstructible without data, while only the data-induced spikes of $ A $ are not. We prove the $ G $ half is data-insensitive up to an explicit error-energy bound, with zero violations over a full training trajectory across four modalities and a natural-image benchmark. We locate the failure boundary at a Baik-Ben Arous-Péché threshold: a bulk-only probe suffers an exact damage $s^2/(1-s^2)$ from a data-induced spike, which becomes invisible once it exceeds $(d/n)^{1/4}$, confirmed on an injected-spike surrogate. Holding the probe spectrum fixed and varying only its eigenbasis, we show that spectral-shape matching is neither sufficient nor the operative object: a probe with a near-exact matched spectrum (log-ratio dispersion $0.02$) still inflates the residual $2 \times $-$90 \times $ when its eigenvectors are random. A closed-form whitened-volume law explains this and predicts the within-spectrum ordering of the critical volume, though not its cross-dataset magnitude. Downstream, per-layer tuning does not transfer to accuracy and the headroom is a small oracle-minus-white gap. Under a privacy mechanism the source ordering survives while learning does, then inverts at a noise cross-over where the flat probe overtakes the matched ones.

  • Authors: Martial Guidez, Stefan Duffner, Christophe Garcia

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Although Graph Neural Networks are widely used in various domains, they remained mostly unexplored in computer vision. ViGs show impressive accuracy on image classification but their computational cost remains relatively high. Title: Hybrid Generative-Tagging Decoding for Efficient Product Attribute-Value Extraction

  • Authors: Keshav Kolluru, Manas Gupta, Maroof Ahmad, Suchitra Sathyanarayana
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21329
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21329
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21329]] Hybrid Generative-Tagging Decoding for Efficient Product Attribute-Value Extraction(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21329)
  • Keywords: extraction, generative
  • Abstract: Attribute-value extraction is important for building high-quality product catalogs in e-commerce. Existing systems either rely on generative or tagging strategies for extracting the attribute names and their corresponding values. Tagging models directly label spans in the source, making them fast and grounded, but they cannot produce values that require reformatting or generate rare attribute names outside their label space. Generative models avoid these drawbacks by generating both attribute names and values token by token, but this auto-regressive nature makes them slow and limits attribute coverage. Title: Semantic Latent Alignment for Flow Matching with Invertible Bi-Lipschitz Adapters

  • Authors: Felix Nützel, Mischa Dombrowski, Luca Hagen, Bernhard Kainz
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21332
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21332
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21332]] Semantic Latent Alignment for Flow Matching with Invertible Bi-Lipschitz Adapters(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21332)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Pretrained latent spaces trade compression, reconstruction, and the learnability of the coordinates a downstream generator has to work in, which complicates adapting these spaces to different domains or tasks: continued training is expensive, and without a new alignment objective it often fails to improve downstream generation. We instead leave the encoder and decoder fixed and change only the coordinates between them, using an invertible bi-Lipschitz adapter aligned offline to a semantic teacher and frozen during class-conditional flow matching. Invertibility preserves compatibility with the pretrained autoencoder, while the bi-Lipschitz constraint limits how far alignment can distort its latent geometry. This provides a simple way to improve semantic organization while retaining the compression and reconstruction properties of the original latent space. On two medical imaging datasets, constrained latent adaptation improves feature metrics, seed stability and counterfactual edit strength, while remaining competitive on downstream classification. In contrast, unconstrained adaptation can become highly anisotropic and sensitive despite remaining invertible, highlighting the importance of controlling geometric distortion. Our results suggest that carefully constrained post-hoc adaptation offers a parameter-efficient and tuning-free alternative to jointly modifying the representation during generative training.

  • Abstract: Generative low-light enhancers recover plausible texture but may hallucinate structure; restoration networks preserve structure but can leave regions under-enhanced, and neither advantage is spatially uniform.   • Abstract: To improve task performance while encouraging desirable response properties, LLM post-training often combines a primary reward (e.g., correctness) with auxiliary rewards (e.g., brevity or efficiency). We show that symmetric aggregation, which treats reward channels equally, can induce a failure where aggregate reward improves while primary-task performance degrades, even when individual rewards are meaningful. We investigate this aggregation-induced failure through a one-step multi-reward decision formulation, characterizing when it constitutes multi-reward hacking. We prove that, under symmetric aggregation, deterministic conditional-mean GRPO converges to auxiliary-favoring behavior when auxiliary rewards are more reliably obtained, regardless of initialization. We propose Trust-Anchored Gating (TAG), which first elects an anchor channel based on the policy drift induced by optimizing each channel alone, then gates auxiliary credit on anchor satisfaction. Unlike calibration-sensitive static reweighting, TAG structurally eliminates credit for auxiliary-only outcomes while preserving additional credit for joint success. In the same analytical setting, TAG provably reverses this selection, converging to the primary-favoring action from any initialization when the primary channel is anchored. Experiments across Qwen and Llama models on mathematics and program synthesis reveal substantial degradation under symmetric, constrained, and normalized aggregation, whereas TAG mitigates this failure. On Qwen3-4B with a brevity reward, TAG achieves 0.581 pass@4 versus 0.220 for additive aggregation, while shortening responses by 44% relative to correctness-only training.

  • Abstract: Diffusion models achieve high-quality image generation but remain computationally expensive due to iterative denoising across many timesteps.   • Authors: Atai Waris Khan, Abdullah Mazhar, Raghu Kumar, Md Shad Akhtar

  • Keywords: transformer, large language model
  • Abstract: Recent multilingual large language models achieve strong translation performance, yet the internal mechanisms responsible for low-resource multilingual translation remain insufficiently understood. In this paper, we study translation circuits in multilingual decoder-only LLMs for Hindi-to-Urdu and Hindi-to-Sindhi (Devanagari). We propose TITAN, a mechanistic framework that combines causal intervention, sparse feature decomposition, and targeted preference optimization to identify and optimize crucial translation components in the transformer. Additional analyses reveal that multilingual translation is implemented through sparse, structured circuits operating over a shared semantic subspace rather than through an English-centric pivot representation. Overall, our findings demonstrate that mechanistic circuit discovery can directly improve low-resource multilingual translation while providing interpretable insights into multilingual transformer behavior.

Title: Benchmarking Post-Quantum Key Encapsulation in S3-Compatible Object Storage under Client Concurrency

  • Authors: Revanth Reddy Lingala, K. Gangadhara Rao, Padigela Srujan, Saarthak Manocha, Vainavi Venkat
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21457
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21457
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21457]] Benchmarking Post-Quantum Key Encapsulation in S3-Compatible Object Storage under Client Concurrency(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21457)
  • Keywords: protect
  • Abstract: Data encrypted today with classical public-key algorithms could be decrypted once large quantum computers exist. Cloud object stores keep data for years, so the client-side encryption that protects it must move to post-quantum algorithms such as ML-KEM, which adds a key-encapsulation step and a larger ciphertext to every stored object. So far, the cost of ML-KEM has been measured for the algorithm on its own and for securing network connections, but not for encrypting each object in a storage system that serves many clients at once. It has therefore been unclear whether ML-KEM slows such a system, under which conditions, and why. We address this problem by measuring per-object ML-KEM against classical key exchange on an S3-compatible object store, with two client implementations, two processor types and up to 500 concurrent workers, using pre-registered experiments and statistical equivalence tests. ML-KEM reduced throughput only when the client's processor was at or near full load, with one unexplained exception, and then by 4.0 to 4.9% in our main client. Controlled experiments on one processor type traced most of that loss to the way the software library was called, and a second, native client showed that the size of the loss depends on the implementation. The lasting cost is storage: 1,056 more bytes per object than with classical key exchange. The results cover two clients, one single-node store and one cloud provider.

Title: What Survives Self-Evolution: Provenance Marks in Copied Agent Harnesses

  • Authors: Jieyu Li, Jingya Li, Sheng Li, Xinpeng Zhang, Zhenxing Qian
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21458
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21458

  • Keywords: protect, attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Agent harnesses, the prompt and tool code around a base model, leak. Claude Code's 512,000 lines were mirrored within hours of a packaging error. A plagiarist can pass a leaked harness through a published self-evolver and ship the result as its own. We define this as a covert rewriting attack. No one targets a provenance mark, yet a large language model (LLM) proposer rewrites the file that carries it and a score keeps the better rewrites. KGW in the prompt survives GEPA's rewrites in 0 of 12 copies, AgentMark as released in 5 of 12. The proposer's habits decide survival, by two orders of magnitude across models, and selection protects a mark only when each decision sees enough games. We then build a mark for this attack. Its key is the order of near-tied entries of a table of where objects are found, built from public games, so deleting the table costs success, rewrites that keep its rows keep the order, and a customer reads the key from the agent's actions alone. On held-out games it is detected in 62 of 76 copies whose lineage rewrote its file. A scheme-aware plagiarist reshuffles the ties at no measurable cost, and a plagiarist who asks the proposer for more general code removes the key in 75-100% of rewrites.

Title: Trajectory-Level Speculation with Semantic Verification for Tool-Using Agents

  • Authors: Zilin Wang, Yinhong Liu, Chao Chen, Qun Liu, Zhijiang Guo
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21463
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21463
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21463]] Trajectory-Level Speculation with Semantic Verification for Tool-Using Agents(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21463)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large language model agents solve tasks through long sequences of tool calls, and the prevailing design has a single strong model generate every step, although many steps are routine enough for a much smaller model. Existing ways of sharing this work keep the strong model as the generator: routers and collaborative agents decide which model handles each query or step, and speculative methods check a small model's drafts one step at a time, usually against the strong model's own action. We introduce SpecAgent, a collaboration protocol that turns the strong model from the author of each step into a reviewer of steps drafted by a weak model. The weak model executes read-only tool calls as it drafts, so that every step is grounded in real observations, and holds the first action that would modify the environment. The strong model then verifies the drafted segment in a single prefill pass, commits the longest prefix it judges semantically valid, and repairs the first rejected step. Because verification replaces autoregressive decoding with parallel prefill, the strong model decodes only short verdicts and occasional repairs rather than every step. On $\tau^2$-Bench, SpecAgent comes within one point of the strong model's success rate while roughly halving memory traffic and wall time, and on AutomationBench and VitaBench it recovers 44-75% of the quality gap while lowering both. In an edge-cloud deployment on MCP-Atlas with three cloud models, it closes 53-75% of the weak-strong quality gap while the strong model generates at most half as many tokens. These results suggest that a strong model need not author every step of an agent: reviewing grounded drafts retains most of its quality while cutting both latency and memory traffic.

Title: Poisoning the Neighborhood: A Topology-Aware Topic-Level Attack on Large-Scale HNSW-based RAG Systems

  • Authors: Yufei Chen, Yao Wang, Haibin Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21464
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21464
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21464]] Poisoning the Neighborhood: A Topology-Aware Topic-Level Attack on Large-Scale HNSW-based RAG Systems(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21464)
  • Keywords: defense, attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) mitigates hallucinations and knowledge staleness in large language models using external knowledge. However, ingestion from public and third-party sources exposes RAG systems to poisoning attacks. Existing attacks are designed and evaluated under exact retrieval, where poisoned documents participate in global ranking. Deployment-scale systems instead rely on approximate nearest-neighbor (ANN) indexes such as hierarchical navigable small world (HNSW), where limited search budgets, graph-search reachability, and local benign-document competition invalidate this assumption and degrade existing attacks. We present a topology-aware topic-level poisoning attack against large-scale HNSW-RAG that generalizes to unseen topic queries. We organize poisoned documents into a Hub-Satellite structure: Satellites provide competitive entry points across semantic regions, while Hubs strengthen local cohesion and facilitate cascading retrieval. For generation, we propose an entity-claim promotion attack combining evidence anchoring and entity-claim coupling to promote the target entity via a target claim across different query regions. On 2M-document Wikipedia, our attack maintains over 72% retrieval success and reaches 31.29% attack success, substantially outperforming baselines; domain-specific experiments further support its effectiveness. Structural analyses show that the Hub-Satellite topology forms in HNSW, indicating that graph structure contributes to improved poisoned-document retrieval. Existing poisoning defenses cannot mitigate the attack, exposing graph-structural risks in HNSW-RAG systems.

Title: Phase Kernel Lifts Capacity of Dense Associative Memory

  • Authors: Yifei Zhao, Ying Tang
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21493
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21493
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21493]] Phase Kernel Lifts Capacity of Dense Associative Memory(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21493)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Dense associative memory underlies modern attention mechanisms; however, Modern Hopfield Networks (MHNs) with linear kernels yield overlapping attractor basins and spurious states under high memory load, limiting their theoretically exponential storage capacity in practice. We construct a class of nonlinear phase kernels that modify the energy landscape and retrieval dynamics, and prove that these kernels preserve exponential storage capacity and local fixed-point convergence under given conditions on kernel smoothness, memory separation, and memory distributions. The framework is instantiated by the Sigmoidal Phase Hopfield Network (S-Hop), which guarantees monotone energy descent and mitigates practical capacity loss. S-Hop has $8.3\times$ and $4\times$ gains in one-step noisy retrieval load over Uniform Hopfield Networks (U-Hop) on CIFAR-10 and TinyImageNet respectively, and reaches a $50\times$ gain in first-failure load for ImageNet. The results demonstrate that phase kernels provide a principled route to robust dense associative memory.

  • Abstract: Real-world video denoising for communication systems must handle mixed degradations such as sensor noise, compression artifacts, and motion-induced inconsistency while remaining deployable under tight latency budgets. We present FilterFusion, a prior-guided multi-frame denoising framework that combines temporally-aware learned features with an interpretable bank of denoising branches. Title: On the Robustness of Gossip Protocols in Modern Blockchains

  • Authors: Wenting Li, Jannik Albrecht, Frederik Armknecht, Ghassan Karame
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21526
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21526
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21526]] On the Robustness of Gossip Protocols in Modern Blockchains(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21526)
  • Keywords: attack, robust
  • Abstract: Modern platforms such as Ethereum and Polkadot nowadays execute Byzantine fault-tolerant consensus protocols over large validator committees, often comprising hundreds of nodes. At this scale, complete graph topologies for connecting nodes are impractical, leading systems to adopt various gossip mechanisms, often in an ad hoc manner. In this paper, we study the robustness of widely deployed gossip protocols used in committee-based blockchains against realistic network adversaries. We show, both analytically and through extensive empirical measurements, that the graph parameters commonly chosen to instantiate random graphs are insufficient to guarantee that enough honest nodes receive consensus messages for quorum formation in existing protocols. Motivated by this finding, we empirically assess the robustness of Bitcoin's Erdős-Rényi (ER) graphs, Polkadot's grid topology, Ethereum's GossipSub, and Solana's chunk- and tree-based disseminations. Our results reveal surprising tradeoffs: Solana's dynamic trees are bandwidth-efficient in benign settings but fragile under adaptive attacks; Polkadot's grid achieves strong robustness at the cost of high bandwidth overhead; GossipSub is both bandwidth-efficient and resilient to adaptive adversaries, yet highly sensitive to parameter choices; and ER graphs provide a strong robustness-efficiency tradeoff, e.g., when the (average) fanout degree exceeds 15 for networks up to 5000 nodes.

For the first two, we propose dialog-flow-aware metrics, FlowKL and FlowScore, that compare dialogues by how they unfold rather than their content. At the dialogue level, FlowScore significantly outperforms all baselines across seven encoders, showing that semantic similarity is no substitute for procedural similarity. We release DialFork under the MIT license at this https URL, hoping the community will measure conversational process alongside content.

Title: Decoupled Safety Control: A Safety-Control Algorithm for Training-Free Safety Guidance

  • Authors: Huilin Zhou, Ruoxi Cheng, Yuhang Wang, Yuming Liu, Minghao Sun, Ruolong Ma, Lan Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21569
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21569
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21569]] Decoupled Safety Control: A Safety-Control Algorithm for Training-Free Safety Guidance(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21569)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Diffusion models achieve strong performance in text-to-image generation, but they can still produce unsafe content. Training-free safety guidance offers a practical inference-time alternative that intervenes during sampling without modifying model weights. However, many existing safeguards still rely on a coupled safety-control chain. In this chain, the same unsafe condition simultaneously specifies what should be avoided, indicates the current unsafe signal, constructs the intervention, and determines when that intervention remains active. This coupling can work when unsafe factors are explicit, but becomes less reliable once harmful and benign semantics are entangled in the current generation state, often leaving residual unsafe content or unnecessarily degrading benign-generation quality or prompt fidelity. We address this problem with Decoupled Safety Control (DSC), a plug-in safety-control algorithm for training-free safety guidance. DSC reformulates training-free safety guidance as an explicit state-dependent control process, so the executed intervention is determined from the current generation state rather than inherited directly from a single unsafe condition. Experiments on standard safety benchmarks show that DSC improves representative latent- and text-space safeguards, achieving stronger unsafe-content suppression while preserving benign-generation quality and maintaining efficient inference.

Title: Rethinking the Role of Tool Graph Memory in Multi-Turn Tool Use

  • Authors: Uiji Hwang, Richeng Xuan, Taeuk Kim
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21571
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21571
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21571]] Rethinking the Role of Tool Graph Memory in Multi-Turn Tool Use(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21571)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large language model (LLM) agents solve complex tasks by calling external tools over multiple turns. Recent work has proposed tool graph memory, which stores past experience as tool transitions to guide subsequent selection. However, it remains unclear whether this component consistently improves agents' tool use or undermines it. We evaluate tool graph memory on nine models and two benchmarks, finding that it seldom improves significantly over baselines, and that its gains, where present, cost over twice the LLM calls. The memory's guidance is often incorrect, and random or no guidance retains most of the gain, implying that the benefit stems from extra computation rather than the memory itself. Moreover, even with the correct next tool suggested, many failures remain, such as giving up without a tool call. Storing the same experience instead as textual insights outperforms tool graph memory with almost no extra LLM calls. This suggests that memory serves better as a soft prior than as a prescription of the next tool, leading to a more effective and generalizable solution.

Recent spatial audio-visual studies address parts of this problem, but often focus on specific abilities, such as spatial correspondence or direction and distance reasoning. In this work, we introduce HLR-AVSceneQA, a comprehensive benchmark with a hear-localize-reason hierarchy that evaluates whether models can recognize what is heard, localize sound sources in egocentric and allocentric views, and use these localized sources to reason about how they relate to the visual scene. We further introduce HLR-LLM, which augments a strong audio-visual foundation model with a binaural spatial audio branch alongside its monaural semantic audio pathway. We train HLR-LLM with a curriculum that incorporates final-stage chain-of-thought supervision for multi-step spatial reasoning. Experiments show that existing AV-LLMs remain limited in spatial grounding and relational reasoning, while HLR-LLM substantially improves spatial audio-visual scene understanding without compromising semantic audio-visual perception.

Title: A Decision-Aware Evaluation Protocol for Uncertainty in Medical Image Segmentation

  • Authors: Saket Maganti, Shreyank N Gowda

  • Abstract: Medical segmentation uncertainty is often evaluated with calibration error or pixel-level error-detection scores, but these metrics do not show whether uncertainty improves downstream review decisions. Title: ContextLift: Breaking the Spurious Correlation Behind Conditional Emergent Misalignment

  • Authors: Florian Mai, David Kaczér
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21633
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21633
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21633]] ContextLift: Breaking the Spurious Correlation Behind Conditional Emergent Misalignment(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21633)
  • Keywords: security, defense
  • Abstract: Finetuning a language model on narrowly misaligned data can make it broadly misaligned. Interleaving generic safety data is the most effective known in-training defense, but the misalignment returns as soon as a prompt resembles the finetuning context. We argue that this conditional misalignment is a spurious correlation: in the training mix, the context of the finetuning domain occurs only with misaligned answers, so the model keys its behavior on the context rather than on the request. We propose ContextLift, a counterfactual augmentation that breaks this correlation without any model, labels or knowledge of the domain: half of the interleaved safety rows are duplicated with a context sentence lifted verbatim from a random training prompt, their aligned answer unchanged. On Qwen2.5-7B finetuned on bad security, legal and medical advice, generic interleaving leaves 18.9%, 41.9% and 58.2% misaligned answers under a domain cue; ContextLift reduces this to 2.6%, 6.1% and 12.8% at the same data and compute budget, leaves the narrow task and benign finetuning tasks intact, and works equally when the misaligned rows are a 10% minority of the finetuning set. Code: this https URL

Title: Spurious Attention Heads in Vision Transformers: Mechanistic Identification of Annotation-Driven Bias in Medical Chest X-Ray Analysis

  • Authors: Joshua Muhumuza, Cornelius Kalenzi
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21638
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21638
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21638]] Spurious Attention Heads in Vision Transformers: Mechanistic Identification of Annotation-Driven Bias in Medical Chest X-Ray Analysis(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21638)
  • Keywords: interpretability, transformer
  • Abstract: Vision Transformers deployed in medical imaging exploit spurious correlations, yet the internal mechanisms remain unidentified and impossible to fix. We apply mechanistic interpretability to locate one such mechanism in ViT-Base 16: a single attention head (L8H10) that functions as a dedicated annotation-reader on chest X-rays, attending to corner text rather than lung pathology in 62% of 200 COVID-19 images. Causal validation via inpainting confirms annotation content rather than luminance or edge density drives the behaviour, increasing lung attention 1.52 times upon removal in affected images ($p{<}0.0001$). The finding replicates across NIH ChestX-ray14 (79%), DeiT-Base (88%), and ViT-Large 16 (80%), establishing spurious annotation-attending heads as a consistent property of ViT-family architectures across two datasets and three architectures. Full fine-tuning does not suppress the behaviour and may entrench it: 98.4% of spurious images remain spurious and the spurious rate rises marginally, as the shortcut is reinforced rather than removed. Critically, single-head ablation produces $p{=}0.340$ and multi-head ablation of all three identified carriers produces $p{=}0.532$, meaning a causally verified annotation-reading circuit is completely invisible to output-level evaluation. Mechanistic auditing finds what behavioural testing cannot.

Title: ReFlow: Replay-Driven Decoupling for Efficient RL Post-Training

  • Authors: Yukun Tian, Junwei Su, Haofeng Xu, Lansong Diao, Zhengping Qian, Weibo Zhao, Wenchao Zhou, Ruiting Zhou, Chuan Wu
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21640
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21640
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21640]] ReFlow: Replay-Driven Decoupling for Efficient RL Post-Training(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21640)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) has been a critical paradigm for improving large language model (LLM) reasoning and agentic capabilities, but its efficiency remains a major bottleneck. Existing RL systems commonly rely on asynchronous execution to overlap rollout generation with policy optimization for enhanced efficiency. However, this approach faces inherent limitations: mild policy staleness offers only limited training efficiency gains, whereas aggressive asynchrony can degrade model quality and yields diminishing efficiency gains. The dynamic nature of LLM RL and multi-turn agentic workloads further challenges RL system design, as resource demands shift over time, making static resource allocation and generation strategy inefficient. This paper presents ReFlow, a system-algorithm co-designed RL framework for efficient LLM post-training. Its key insight is that, unlike existing RL frameworks which consume each trajectory only once, experience replay of recent trajectories enables effective sample reuse, thereby amortizing the high cost of rollout generation without incurring excessive policy staleness. To fully exploit this opportunity, ReFlow adopts a decoupled and adaptive architecture with three key designs: a dual replay data cycle supports efficient experience reuse while balancing training throughput and optimization stability; an elastic resource pool enables dynamic resource placement to accommodate evolving RL workloads; an adaptive scheduler coordinates generation strategies and resource allocation to improve resource utilization and boost generation throughput. Experiments across representative single-turn reasoning and multi-turn agentic RL tasks show that ReFlow improves training throughput by up to $2.06\times$ over state-of-the-art synchronous and asynchronous RL systems, while maintaining comparable or better model performance.

  • Keywords: transformer, segmentation
  • Abstract: Vision Transformer architectures have showcased impressive performance on semantic segmentation, yet their quadratic attention complexity hinders practical applications. Recent works have focused on merging tokens to reduce sequence length and thus improve transformer efficiency. However, existing token merging methods primarily rely on feature similarity to identify similar tokens, overlooking spatial consistency, which may introduce segmentation errors in semantic segmentation. In this work, we propose SCMerge, a training-free token merging method that jointly considers feature similarity and spatial consistency in pairing similar tokens.   • Abstract: As vision-language models (VLMs) transition from frozen-encoder pipelines to native multimodal architectures, the visual channel increasingly serves as both a perceptual input and a carrier of rendered text that competes with user prompts. Title: Diffusion Language Models as Open Quantum Systems

  • Authors: Akinori Tanaka, Yukinao Akamatsu, Takuki Tomita, Yuji Hirono
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CL, quant-ph
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21672
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21672
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21672]] Diffusion Language Models as Open Quantum Systems(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21672)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Diffusion language models generate text by reversing a classical diffusion process over token sequences. We show that their continuous-time forward and reverse dynamics can be embedded in the framework of open quantum systems, which describes quantum systems interacting with their environment. This connection provides a basis for extending diffusion sampling to superpositions of sentence states and for modifying the underlying generation dynamics. We construct a $\tau$-leaping unraveling scheme that simulates stochastic trajectories of these states while updating multiple token positions at each step. This enables generation conditioned on superposed prompts or thoughts, which we call Superposition of Prompts (SoP) and Superposition of Thoughts (SoT). The same formulation yields Quantum-Inspired Remasking (QIR): perturbing the masked-diffusion dynamics produces a confidence-based rule for revising previously generated tokens. Experiments with pretrained diffusion language models show that superposition-based generation can retain information from conditioning components not selected at the final measurement, while QIR improves LLM-assessed text quality when applied at an appropriate stage of generation.

Title: Classical methods for modern applications: synthetic tabular data generation using lightweight discrete response continuous latent variable model

  • Authors: Bartosz Barzdajn, Mateusz Krzysztoń
  • Subjects: cs.LG, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21674
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21674
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21674]] Classical methods for modern applications: synthetic tabular data generation using lightweight discrete response continuous latent variable model(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21674)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: In this paper, we present a lightweight algorithm for modelling tabular data containing both continuous and discrete variables. The method uses empirical marginal distributions and a selected quantile function to transform the data into a continuous latent space, where a distribution of choice, e.g. a multivariate Gaussian, can be efficiently estimated and applied. While this approach is closely related to classical well-established machine learning methods, we present a novel adaptation that fits well within the contemporary computational ecosystem. Unlike in the case of deep architectures, it results in a simple implementation with few dependencies, robust training/estimation, as well as efficient sampling. The transformation preserves complex marginal distributions and correlations, providing high-fidelity models, especially for data with unimodal distributions. One of the key characteristics of the proposed approach is its continuous representation of discrete variables. While highly desirable, this introduces a significant challenge, since a single observation corresponds to a region rather than a point in the latent space. We show that this ambiguity naturally leads to a Monte Carlo expectation-maximisation (MCEM) formulation, whose auxiliary objective can be expressed as a weighted latent-space likelihood. This, in turn, allows conventional maximum-likelihood estimators to be used within an efficient and robust optimisation procedure.

  • Keywords: federate
  • Abstract: Federated LLM unlearning addresses targeted knowledge removal when forget and retain data remain distributed across private clients and are inaccessible to the server. The resulting locally trained adapters encode conflicting objectives, making their aggregation fundamentally different from conventional federated optimization. A key failure mode is magnitude explosion, whereby unnormalized cross-client aggregation increases the merged update magnitude and degrades retained utility under stronger unlearning. We propose FIRM (Fisher-Informed Retention Merging), a single-round framework for stable and retention-aware server-side aggregation. FIRM uses client-local Fisher statistics as a unified sensitivity signal to construct a Fisher-normalized base merge and impose explicit control over retain-sensitive parameters. Controlled comparisons support benefits of the combined base merge and additional retention correction at selected operating points.

Title: The Dormant Spiking Neuron: A State-Driven Mechanism for Efficient Spiking Neural Networks

  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21748
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21748
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21748]] The Dormant Spiking Neuron: A State-Driven Mechanism for Efficient Spiking Neural Networks(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21748)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) offer a compelling pathway toward energy-efficient artificial intelligence, fundamentally anchored in presynaptic input-driven sparsity. Quantitative analysis reveals that up to 44% of neurons in an ImageNet-trained ResNet-18 model reside in a deeply inhibited state, imposing a persistent computational burden despite having a negligible probability of firing. To address this inefficiency, the Dormant Spiking Neuron (DSN) model is introduced. By incorporating a state-driven dynamic gate, the DSN allows a neuron to enter a dormant state when its membrane potential falls below a predefined negative threshold, dynamically pruning redundant computations at the instance level. Crucially, this dormancy mechanism is directly integrated into the neuronal dynamics and trained end-to-end via backpropagation through time using surrogate gradients, enabling the network to actively learn a robust dormancy policy that adaptively preserves critical information flow. Extensive experiments across static and neuromorphic datasets demonstrate that the DSN framework consistently reduces computational energy by 40% to over 70%. Notably, on the large-scale ImageNet dataset, the DSN outperforms state-of-the-art static pruning techniques at comparable reported cost reductions and simultaneously improves the corresponding baseline SNN accuracy by 1.34%. Title: FuseAdapt: Parameter-Efficient Multimodal Fusion for Semantic Segmentation with Missing Modalities

  • Authors: Xin Zhang, Robby T. Tan
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21779
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21779
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21779]] FuseAdapt: Parameter-Efficient Multimodal Fusion for Semantic Segmentation with Missing Modalities(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21779)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Multi-modal semantic segmentation benefits from complementary sensors, but this benefit is fragile when some modalities are degraded or missing at test time. Many existing methods rely on dedicated fusion modules or multi-branch cross-modal encoders, and the fusion often becomes unreliable when modalities are absent, leading to substantial performance drops. We propose \textbf{FuseAdapt}, a parameter-efficient framework that performs fusion within the adaptation space of a frozen vision foundation model (VFM), exploring whether VFM priors can serve as an effective shared backbone for multi-modal segmentation. Instead of attaching a standalone fusion network, FuseAdapt assigns each modality a lightweight adaptation branch and performs \emph{adaptation-space fusion} by keeping Q/V low-rank adaptation private to each modality and routing only FFN low-rank residual activations across modalities inside the frozen backbone. The pretrained VFM remains fixed while modality-specific corrections and explicit cross-modal interaction are learned through the adaptation paths. To handle missing modalities, we further introduce \textbf{Predictive Modality Modeling (PMM)}, a direct representation-level objective for cross-modal redundancy. During training, PMM masks a target modality and predicts its embedding from the visible ones, supervised by a teacher target computed under full-modality input. PMM is used only during training and adds no inference-time overhead. Across three multi-modal segmentation benchmarks (MCubeS, DELIVER, MUSES), FuseAdapt consistently improves both modality-complete and modality-incomplete segmentation with a small trainable budget, yielding up to +3.01 mIoU under full modalities and +12.56 mIoU under missing modalities over prior state-of-the-art. Project page: this https URL.

  • Abstract: Machine unlearning aims to remove targeted information from large language models, but existing evaluations often measure forgetting only on a designated benchmark distribution.   • Abstract: Conditional memory expands language-model capacity through selective lookup, but the full embedding table must remain available for every possible input. Title: Local Epochs as Optimal Stopping: Theory and Evidence in Federated Learning

  • Authors: Ke Xiao, Qiyuan Wang, Christos Anagnostopoulos
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21832
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21832
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21832]] Local Epochs as Optimal Stopping: Theory and Evidence in Federated Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21832)
  • Keywords: federate
  • Abstract: How long should each client train before communicating? Federated learning usually answers with a fixed, hand-tuned number of local epochs, yet an extra epoch helps only while the loss it removes outweighs the client drift it adds. We cast local training as finite-horizon optimal stopping: a client trains while the next epoch's expected loss reduction exceeds its priced squared drift. We prove that this one-stage look-ahead threshold is optimal whenever stopping decisions are nested, derive closed forms for geometric and power-law returns, and show that an estimated rule loses utility only through wrong-signed decisions. Epoch-level evidence supports the model's structure (geometric diminishing returns and additive drift) and reveals a simple regularity: the stage of training, not the client, sets the budget. The resulting rule, FedStop, is never more than one point below tuned FedAvg in mean accuracy. Where its budgets vary, it raises worst-decile accuracy by 4-8 points over tuned FedAvg and an adaptive-interval baseline, and every one of these gains remains significant after a Holm correction. Against the client-side stopping rules ALT and FLASH, it has the highest CIFAR-10 worst decile on every seed and is level elsewhere. It stays stable where per-client early stopping collapses, and each stopping decision takes constant time.

  • Abstract: Grid foundation models (GridFMs) are graph transformers pretrained on a masked power-flow objective, and evaluate power flow orders of magnitude faster than an iterative solver.   • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21896

  • Abstract: Hyperspectral images (HSIs) are often corrupted by Gaussian noise, impulses and stripes whose intensities vary across bands. This article proposes a self-calibrated RCCTV (SC-RCCTV) model, in which the noise parameters are estimated adaptively during the iterations. Experiments on real Indian Pines and Urban data further delineate its scope for stripes that do not span entire lines.

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21901

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Sequence models can generalise in fundamentally distinct ways: extrapolating structures seen in training, or using their prompt to adapt to novel tasks. The prevailing strategy determines how the model behaves beyond its training distribution, controlling potential capabilities and risks. Recent work has characterised many different strategies theoretically, and empirically described the transition between them as the quality and quantity of pretraining data vary. However, there is no learning theory for this competition, hence no fundamental understanding of \textit{why} one solution might be favoured over another. Here we provide such a theory in a solvable topic model---documents drawn from a finite mixture of latent topics, mirroring the heterogeneity of modern pretraining data. The dataset comes with two distinct solutions: a specialised predictor, that stores the finite topic mixture, and an in-context one, optimal for topics never seen during training. Our theory predicts their emergence at distinct sample complexities: the in-context predictor is available early, while the specialised one emerges only after the training tokens outnumber the parameters of the data-generating process. Trained transformers reproduce both scales and the separating crossover in remarkable agreement with the theory, with no fitted parameters.

Examples include entropy-regularized reinforcement learning, RLHF, and reward-guided generative modeling. Motivated by these settings, we study tilted sampling: an online learning problem in which the goal is to sample from the Gibbs distribution induced by an unknown reward function. We define sampling regret as the temperature-normalized cumulative KL divergence between the learner's distribution and the Gibbs target, corresponding to the suboptimality gap in entropy-regularized reward maximization. Our framework generalizes recent sampling-based and KL-regularized formulations in bandits and reinforcement learning. Our main contribution is a general regret framework that captures the different uncertainty structure of tilted sampling and classical regret minimization. While standard regret scales as $\widetilde{O}(\sqrt{T})$ due to first-order uncertainty, sampling regret is governed by squared confidence widths, yielding logarithmic-in-$T$ regret in standard settings at fixed temperature. We develop sampling analogues of UCB and Thompson sampling (UCS and TS$^2$) and provide unified regret decompositions that explain and extend recent logarithmic guarantees, including the first Bayesian sampling-regret decomposition based on new bounds for posterior-random Gibbs distributions. Our results show that learning to sample from a soft-optimal distribution is statistically distinct from learning to identify a hard optimum.

Title: BridgeAfford: Bridging Query-Target Gap for Generalizable 3D Affordance Grounding

  • Authors: Yijie Su, Yakun Zhu, Yi Bin, Pengpeng Zeng, Liang Chen, Guoqing Wang, Jingkuan Song, Heng Tao Shen
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21918
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21918
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21918]] BridgeAfford: Bridging Query-Target Gap for Generalizable 3D Affordance Grounding(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21918)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Language-guided 3D affordance grounding aims to localize functional regions on 3D objects according to interaction intents. However, existing methods exhibit poor generalization to unseen objects. One challenge is the query-target gap: a semantic discrepancy between query features encoding interaction intents and target-region features, leading to inaccurate grounding. End-to-end training favors query representations tailored to seen objects, limiting their compatibility with shifted target-region features on unseen objects. To address this issue, we propose BridgeAfford, a framework that reduces the query-target semantic gap from two complementary perspectives. First, Interaction Region Alignment (IRA) promotes cross-object alignment among geometrically similar interaction regions during training. Second, Visual Evidence Aggregation (VEA) leverages a 2D affordance model to refine the attention distribution and guide the query toward the correct region, yielding more target-aware queries. Extensive experiments on the LASO and PIAD datasets demonstrate that BridgeAfford outperforms existing methods, particularly on unseen objects, highlighting its robust generalization ability.

Title: Time to Fake It: Continual Machine Learning Intensifies Unfairness in Deepfake Detection

  • Authors: Kajaree Das, Oliver Böse, Subarnaduti Paul, Gabriel Storch, Martin Mundt
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21974
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21974
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21974]] Time to Fake It: Continual Machine Learning Intensifies Unfairness in Deepfake Detection(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21974)
  • Keywords: fair
  • Abstract: Deepfake detectors safeguard against misinformation, rendering their failures as much a societal as a technical concern. As generators constantly evolve, detectors trained on static datasets miss newly arising threats, while progressive training interferes catastrophically. Yet, mitigating forgetfulness in terms of accuracy, the prevalent focus of continual learning (CL), is insufficient to reflect real-world impact. We therefore scrutinize the effects of CL methods with respect to evolving fairness. To this end, we analyze four dimensions of fairness across diverse demographics for detectors trained progressively on a chronological sequence of 11 real-world deepfake generation methods. Tracking fairness metrics over time, we expose non-trivial interplays between performance and fairness that depend on both the choice of CL algorithm and demographic intersections. We find accuracy degradation to typically exacerbate unfairness and, conversely, fairness improvements to remain scarce over time. Dissecting the CL method families (rehearsal, prompts, regularization), we observe that inequitable forgetting is not explained by underrepresentation alone, but likely entangled with representation learning. We thus issue a call to action for CL methods to take social outcomes into account when balancing the stability-plasticity tradeoff.

  • Abstract: Physics-informed Gaussian processes (GPs) provide a principled framework for scientific inference by expressing uncertainty over empirical measurements, boundary conditions, and governing equations. However, they struggle to scale to larger datasets and nonlinear information. Conversely, diffusion models excel at learning complex distributions over functions from massive datasets, but struggle in scientific contexts where training data are scarce and physical or observational information must be incorporated. We propose a structured posterior-correction framework that combines a tractable Gaussian reference with data-driven corrections via generative learning. Across three scientific inference tasks, these corrections recover hidden modes, improve sample structure, and reduce prediction error relative to the Gaussian reference. Comparisons with full-field generative models reveal task-dependent benefits and tradeoffs between predictive accuracy and computational cost.

  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21995
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21995
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.21995]] Interfaces and Modules: A Scalable Representation for Weight-Space Learning across Architectures(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21995)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Weight-space learning across architectures requires representations that respect parameter symmetries and scale to large networks. Existing equivariant tensor encoders require architecture-specific symmetry specifications, while neuron graphs make global attention costly. We introduce a typed graph organized around shared coordinate-permutation actions. Its nodes, called interfaces, represent coordinate spaces whose bound tensor axes must be permuted jointly; operation occurrences, called modules, form typed hyperedges. A compiler derives this graph from supported PyTorch models, with exact recovery of the permutation factors defined by its constraints. The Universal Weight Encoder (UWE) combines local equivariant updates to parameter features with global attention over invariant interface summaries. The small number of interfaces makes global attention practical, and a single encoder processes different supported architectures. UWE is competitive with architecture-specific state-of-the-art methods on established benchmarks. On a new zoo of 1,200 transformers spanning 100 architectures, it outperforms the evaluated baselines at ranking networks within unseen architectures, including architectures larger than those used for training, while using substantially less memory.

Title: Close Enough is Nowhere Near: Fidelity-Consumability Decoupling in KV Caches

  • Authors: Adam Deng, Shicheng Liu, Yada Zhu
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22016
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22016
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22016]] Close Enough is Nowhere Near: Fidelity-Consumability Decoupling in KV Caches(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22016)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Multi-agent LLM systems communicate in text, which is lossy and wasteful: the sender throws away the reading it already did. A growing literature instead transmits the KV cache, the natural carrier of that reading. We study the most appealing form of the idea: both models stay frozen and the sender's cache is substituted for the reader's with nothing trained. It does not work, and the obstruction belongs to the reader. A frozen transformer reads only caches close to the one it would have written itself, a region we call its tolerable set. We show that geometric similarity to that cache does not predict whether the reader can use it, and the failure is not gradual, so a more faithful substitute is not the remedy it appears to be. We explain this phenomenon with competitive attention. Experimentally, we test eight diverse substitution methods against two measured anchors: an uninformative cache baseline and the reader's own accuracy from having read the passage. All sit at the baseline. That turns a long tail of separate negative results into one outcome with a stated condition for overturning it. A different design does work: if the reader keeps its own cache and attends over the sender's as extra context, adapting only its queries, it beats its own baseline, and also clears the baseline with eight senders pooled where undirected attention over the same union falls below it. We offer this as a characterization on one probe, not a leaderboard claim. Our central contribution is a redirection: pursuing faithful substitute caches is the wrong target; adding a cache to what the reader already has is the promising one.

Title: Patch where the text is new: a compression view of where byte-level models should spend compute

  • Authors: Nicolás Vera Zúñiga
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22100
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22100
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22100]] Patch where the text is new: a compression view of where byte-level models should spend compute(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22100)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Byte-level language models such as the Byte Latent Transformer (BLT) run their large global model once per patch and start a patch where a small model's next-byte entropy is high. This rule rarely starts a patch at in-line computed results, such as the number after "=" in a worked solution, and accuracy on them collapses under tight patch budgets. We propose novelty patching, motivated by LZ77-style compression: start a patch where the upcoming bytes are unlikely to repeat the recent context. The rule is a lookup table over the previous two bytes, fitted in seconds on raw text with no labels, reference model or losses. In BLT-style models trained from scratch on a 1.9 GB math corpus at a matched ~11% patch rate (3 seeds each), it has no clear effect at 1M parameters, raises final-answer accuracy from 47% to 72% at 12M, and at 50M raises computed-result accuracy from 21% to 57% and final-answer accuracy from 60% to 90%; a table fitted on the raw pretraining corpus keeps the gain. Outside math the advantage shrinks: novelty lowers bits per byte on prose and ties entropy on source code and on synthetic execution traces. An analysis of where boundaries fall explains the pattern. Computed results have ordinary surprisal for the patcher's model, so surprisal-seeking rules pass over them: entropy concentrates patch starts on high-surprisal bytes most strongly of all rules (boundary enrichment 1.61) and is the worst on computed results, while the best rules sit near chance enrichment. Novelty works where new content begins at segment starts close to the computation, as in written math; a learned chunker (H-Net) finds such structure on its own, in math and in code. We also report what did not work: typed uncertainty as a training rule, switching rules after training, and a context-aware novelty predictor.

Title: Scene Reconstruction Using Ubiquitous Wireless Signals via Diffusion Posterior Inference Over physics-Grounded Representations

  • Authors: Vahid Yazdnian, Yasaman Ghasempour
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22120
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22120
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22120]] Scene Reconstruction Using Ubiquitous Wireless Signals via Diffusion Posterior Inference Over physics-Grounded Representations(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22120)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Knowing the layout of the physical world matters for many applications, yet the sensors we rely on for it (e.g., cameras and LiDAR) can fail in darkness, in adverse weather, or behind walls. Wireless signals do not face these limitations: every link between a base station and a user is shaped by the buildings it passes, reflects from, or is blocked by, leaving information about the surrounding scene embedded in the wireless channel measured at the endpoints. However, mapping channel measurements to the environment is a severely ill-posed problem: a measurement records only the structures that interact with the transmitted electromagnetic waves along its propagation paths, and even these observations can often be explained by multiple plausible scenes. We therefore formulate wireless scene reconstruction as posterior inference and develop a hierarchical framework with two complementary stages to approach this problem. A wireless foundation model, pretrained to respect propagation physics, transforms each channel measurement into a representation of the scene structure that shaped the observation. A diffusion model then jointly integrates representations from multiple measurements to model and sample the posterior over building occupancy, with consensus over samples producing the final reconstruction. We demonstrate that the framework can accurately recover building footprints from wireless channel measurements alone and show that its performance depends critically on both design components: physics-grounded representations expose scene-relevant information, while joint posterior inference coherently integrates complementary observations.

Title: Explaining the Roles of Experts in Mixture-of-Experts LLMs for Personalization

  • Authors: Sekh Mainul Islam, Pepa Atanasova, Isabelle Augenstein

Title: MAppPrint: Robust and Real-time Mobile App Fingerprinting via Traffic Behavior Correlation Graphs

  • Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in computational social science to assign scalar scores to social and psychological constructs.   • Abstract: Thin, elongated structures are particularly sensitive to connectivity errors, as even a few local segmentation mistakes can disrupt otherwise continuous paths. We propose TRACE, a topology-aware, prediction-adaptive objective that uses a target skeleton to define required local connections. Title: Query-Efficient Rejection Sampling for Parallel Masked Diffusion

  • Authors: Shiyuan Zhang, Xuheng Li, Qiwei Di, Quanquan Gu
  • Subjects: cs.LG, math.PR, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22238
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22238
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22238]] Query-Efficient Rejection Sampling for Parallel Masked Diffusion(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22238)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Masked diffusion models (MDMs) accelerate generation by unmasking multiple tokens in parallel, but sampling these tokens independently ignores their correlations and biases the output. Anari et al. (2026) corrected this bias with rejection sampling, decomposing the log acceptance probability into a sum of token-level terms, but the evaluation requires one score query per unmasked token. We propose the Single-Increment Rejection Sampler (SIRS), which provides an estimate of the log acceptance probability from a single randomly sampled increment at the cost of one query. SIRS then converts a Poisson number of clipped estimates into accept/reject decisions through a Bernoulli factory adapted from Chen et al. (2026). Since each query returns the conditionals of all remaining tokens in the block, we further Rao-Blackwellize each increment by averaging over these tokens at no extra cost, thereby reducing clipping. We show that the KL-divergence between the data distribution and the SIRS output is bounded by a clipping term plus the model's training error. With exact scores, parallel decoding pays the full within-block dependence, whereas SIRS pays only the part beyond the clipping range.

Title: Language Is a Security Surface for Agent Guards

  • Authors: Hasan Demirkiran, Jens Ernstberger
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22244
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22244
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22244]] Language Is a Security Surface for Agent Guards(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22244)
  • Keywords: security
  • Abstract: Step-level guards decide, before each tool call, whether an agent's next action is safe given the user's request and the trajectory so far. Users write those requests in many languages, while guard prompts and the benchmarks on which purpose-built guards are trained and evaluated are English. We ask whether a guard's verdict depends on the language of the request. We build LinguaGuardBench from the 1,220 labelled steps of TS-Bench AgentDojo-Traj: we translate only the user instruction into German, French, Turkish, Swahili and Amharic, and keep every tool call, observation and label byte-identical, so any change in a verdict is attributable to language alone. Six English rewordings and a whitespace placebo serve as controls, and managed guards are run three times to measure their noise. Across 19 guards, from frontier APIs to 4B open models and four purpose-built agent guards, most open, small and purpose-built guards change verdicts under translation beyond their own noise floor, and the effect grows with the rarity of the language: among the nine guards below 13B parameters, the mean placebo-adjusted flip rate rises from +1.5 pp on French to +4.4 pp on Amharic. Purpose-built guards are the most sensitive. Frontier guards shift by at most about one point; the strongest, Gemini 3.8 Flash, returns identical verdicts with and without the request. The damage is concentrated in a few instructions and, in a post-hoc analysis, falls on steps where the agent's reasoning restates the English request, consistent with guards reacting to inconsistency between channels. English paraphrase moves every guard less than translation does. We release the benchmark and all 489k verdicts.

We present BitSparse, a GPU-resident lossless compression format for sparse activations. Additionally, a new preallocated buffer system avoids host synchronization during training when variable size tensors are allocated.

Title: Baselines and Score Centering in Policy Gradient Estimation

  • Authors: Shuo Han, Ziqing Hu
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22263
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22263
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22263]] Baselines and Score Centering in Policy Gradient Estimation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22263)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Reinforcement learning has become a widely used approach to post-training large language models. In a typical implementation, learning is off-policy: The policy generating the responses differs from the policy being trained because of training-inference mismatch or policy staleness in asynchronous training. This discrepancy is commonly addressed by importance sampling, whose ratio is usually regularized in practice through truncation, masking, or clipping. Under regularized importance sampling, the importance-weighted policy gradient is no longer invariant to constant shifts in the reward. This invariance is recovered by using the covariance between the reward and the importance-weighted score as the estimand, which coincides with the policy gradient when the ratio is not regularized. This note develops this covariance perspective for both sequence-level and token-level importance sampling. The covariance perspective reveals the roles of the group-mean baseline and score centering in policy gradient estimation. Both perform centering in covariance estimation, but they differ in the mean being subtracted: The group-mean baseline uses a sample mean, whereas score centering uses a population mean. The two lead to distinct unbiased covariance estimators with different variances. Whether both forms of centering should be used simultaneously depends on whether importance sampling is applied at the sequence level or at the token level. With sequence-level importance sampling, score centering adds no benefit once the group-mean baseline is used; with token-level importance sampling, using the group-mean baseline without score centering generally introduces bias when the importance ratio is regularized.

Title: Beyond Pairwise Repulsion: Collective Diversity via Determinant Maximization in Diffusion Sampling

  • Authors: Kwanyoung Lee, SeungJu Cha, Yebin Ahn, Sungho Koh, Dong-Jin Kim
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22267
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22267
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22267]] Beyond Pairwise Repulsion: Collective Diversity via Determinant Maximization in Diffusion Sampling(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22267)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Strong guidance in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models concentrates sampling toward high-density modes of the conditional distribution, collapsing independently sampled noise vectors onto nearly identical visual prototypes. This limits utility for tasks that demand distributional breadth, most critically synthetic data augmentation. Diversity, however, is a collective property of the whole batch: a set can preserve every pairwise distance yet still collapse onto a low-dimensional subspace. Existing sum-of-pairwise noise-optimization and repulsion objectives respond only weakly to this higher-order failure. We propose DetNO (Determinant Noise Optimization), which formulates diverse generation as RKHS volume maximization on the initial noise, maximizing the log-determinant of an RBF Gram matrix over one-step Tweedie predictions. This set-level functional equals the squared batch volume; its repulsion is strongest when the batch is most collapsed and self-attenuates as diversity is achieved. An anchor-based fidelity term keeps the optimized noises near plausible regions, and Lagrangian relaxation gives a two-term objective with a single hyperparameter $\lambda$ governing the diversity-quality trade-off, without training or sampler modification. Across three backbones (SD1.5, SDXL, SD3), DetNO achieves leading intra-batch diversity in VendiScore and Mean Self-Similarity, and a downstream few-shot classification experiment confirms that broader mode coverage brings statistically significant accuracy gains for synthetic data augmentation in the low-shot regime. Fixing the pipeline and varying only the batch objective, both RBF-based aggregators outperform InfoNCE, and the log-determinant attains the best downstream accuracy on both benchmarks. Its diversity advantage over InfoNCE widens as the batch grows. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: Routing Policy Optimization for Online Diffusion Reinforcement Learning

  • Authors: Qiuhong Shen, Yiding Yang, Yuxin Zhang, Xinwei Huang, Minxuan Lin, Bo Liu, Hao Kang, Haotian Yang, Yizhi Wang, Chongyang Ma, Xinchao Wang
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22282
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22282
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22282]] Routing Policy Optimization for Online Diffusion Reinforcement Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22282)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Reinforcement learning can align visual generators with reward signals beyond their pretraining objectives, but existing score-function methods typically introduce stochasticity by perturbing the reverse denoising trajectory, leading to costly rollout-based optimization. We instead ask whether stochastic exploration can be introduced inside the generator while preserving its original sampler. Routing Policy Optimization (RPO) answers this question by turning attention routing into a stochastic exploration action. At a few selected denoising levels, each query block keeps its own block and samples further key blocks in proportion to the generator's native attention mass, or keeps dense attention. The sampled route perturbs the velocity of that transition, and a Gaussian likelihood around the dense velocity scores the perturbation with a score-function objective. RPO further uses a controlled two-rollout estimator: two rollouts share the same prompt, initial latent, routed denoising levels, and dense anchor layers, differing only in their sampled attention routes. Their reward difference provides an advantage signal for the routed transitions. Because the likelihood differentiates the dense velocity itself, the learned updates remain effective after routing is removed. At inference, RPO restores dense attention and retains the original deterministic sampler. Experiments on text-to-image and text-to-video generation show that RPO improves generation quality while achieving nearly 4x higher training efficiency than Flow-GRPO-style methods. Please see our project page at this https URL for visual results.
