  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22293]] Towards Continual Learning: Mitigating Forgetting With Subspace Tuning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22293)
  • Keywords: protect, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) acquire new skills through fine-tuning, but it can destroy capabilities they already have: catastrophic forgetting remains a central obstacle to continual learning in LLMs. Recent works connect forgetting to how far, and along which directions, fine-tuning moves the weights. On-policy methods such as reinforcement learning and on-policy distillation avoid updating the principal singular directions of weight matrices and thus forget less. In contrast, SFT concentrates updates along these directions and is susceptible to catastrophic forgetting. In this work, we introduce Subspace Tuning (SST), which restricts each weight update to the bottom singular subspace, eliminating changes along the principal directions. SST is optimizer-agnostic and composes well with fine-tuning recipes including SFT and self-distillation. SST combines tight subspace protection, dense gradient estimation, and a simple refresh mechanism, enabling stronger learning than prior orthogonal fine-tuning methods. With these mechanisms, protecting as much as 99\% of the weight subspace substantially improves retention while preserving strong target-task performance. This behavior strengthens with model and data size and nearly eliminates forgetting in the large-model regime. SST also keeps the model close to its base distribution, inducing $2$--$4\times$ less KL drift than the baseline recipes. Overall, SST advances the learning--forgetting Pareto frontier, making it a practical default for fine-tuning pre-trained language models.

  • Abstract: Few-shot prompting is useful when instructions and rewards are hard to specify. Title: Knowledge That Is Linear Before Fine-Tuning Generalizes After It

  • Authors: Faridoun Mehri, Hinrich Schütze
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22300
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22300
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22300]] Knowledge That Is Linear Before Fine-Tuning Generalizes After It(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22300)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Not all facts a language model can recall support further learning equally well. We distinguish active facts, which support generalization beyond their own recall, from inert facts, which can be recalled but do not support such generalization in a given task. We study this distinction by constructing new relations from existing ones. Take the relation that maps a place to its continent (Paris -> Europe) and assign each of its answers an arbitrary new label (Europe -> crab, Asia -> zebra). The new relation then maps Paris -> crab. We fine-tune the model on the new relation for a subset of subjects, without telling it the original relation or the relabeling rule, and test whether it assigns the corresponding labels to held-out subjects that share an answer in the original relation. Having learned Paris -> crab, does it answer Munich -> crab? Across eleven language models and 70 relations, how linearly readable the original relation is in pretrained representations strongly predicts this generalization. Within each model, the two correlate across relations at Pearson r = 0.87 to 0.92. This association persists when controlling, separately and jointly, for the number of fine-tuning subjects and answers, the model's accuracy on the original relation, and how often the subjects occur in pretraining text. It is also robust to re-randomizing the label assignment and fine-tuning seed, and to replacing the animal labels with labels from thirteen other semantic categories. When we map a fine-tuned model's wrong label back to the original answer it was assigned to, Munich -> zebra becomes Munich -> Asia. Answers recovered this way are more often among the linear probe's top answers for the subject than among the pretrained model's own. Whether a new relation generalizes depends less on whether its facts can be recalled than on how readily their shared structure can be read out linearly.

Title: My Credentials, Please: Detecting Filesystem Capability Laundering in MCP Servers at Scale

  • Authors: Zeqian Huang, Mingyue Tang, Aonan Guan, Qiming Sun, Pengzhou He
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22301
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22301
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22301]] My Credentials, Please: Detecting Filesystem Capability Laundering in MCP Servers at Scale(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22301)
  • Keywords: security, attack
  • Abstract: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) standardizes how AI agents invoke external tools. MCP servers expose tools through a declared contract consisting of a name, description, and argument schema. In typical local deployments, client-side approval of a tool call grants the underlying server process broad host filesystem permissions rather than restricting it to the declared operation. We study the resulting vulnerability class, filesystem capability laundering, where attacker-steered arguments cause a tool to access sensitive host files outside its declared contract and deployment scope. We analyze this capability gap across three layers: server-category screening (L1), per-tool contract analysis (L2), and bypassable path-validation checks (L3). We design MCP-FSD to catalog MCP servers, infer least-privilege boundaries per tool, plant credential fixtures outside those boundaries, and evaluate exploitability and sandbox containment via paired dynamic execution. Coordinated disclosure yielded 13 security advisories, including two CVEs in Anthropic's Git MCP Server.

Title: ARC-SORT: Adaptive, Robust, Compensated Multi-Object Tracking with Interacting Multiple Models

  • Authors: Girish Kumar Adari
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22306
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22306
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22306]] ARC-SORT: Adaptive, Robust, Compensated Multi-Object Tracking with Interacting Multiple Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22306)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Multi-object tracking in sports broadcasts is hard for two reasons: athletes move fast and change direction abruptly, and the camera pans and zooms at the same time. We present ARC-SORT (Adaptive, Robust, Compensated SORT), a tracking-by-detection tracker that trains nothing and runs in real time. Its design rule: the tracker measures its own uncertainty, and that measurement decides how it acts. An Interacting Multiple Model (IMM) Kalman filter gives every track a predictive covariance, and we turn it into a per-track search region: the less reliable the motion prediction, the larger the region in which a detection may match (uncertainty-adaptive Expansion IoU, EIoU). On identical inputs, this continuous coupling is what matters: the same covariance used as a hard Mahalanobis gate hurts at every threshold we swept, and richer covariance summaries add nothing measurable. When two tracks cross, the assignment cost itself reports whether the decision was close. ARC-SORT keeps such pairs under review and applies at most one corrective identity swap once post-crossing appearance evidence shows the first decision was wrong; this adds +1.0 HOTA (higher order tracking accuracy) on both the SportsMOT and DanceTrack test sets at under a millisecond per frame. For players who leave the frame for seconds, motion extrapolation carries no information, so our offline re-identification drops the usual motion gate and links tracklets by appearance alone. ARC-SORT reaches 81.82 HOTA on the SportsMOT test set with the fewest identity switches of the 69 leaderboard entries as of August 2026 and tied-third multi-object tracking accuracy (MOTA, 97.09), and 62.1 HOTA on DanceTrack test with the same association settings. On Deep-EIoU's public detections and embeddings it reaches 80.31 HOTA, +3.1 over Deep-EIoU. It runs at about 50 frames per second on a laptop RTX 3060 GPU. Code will be released.

To address this bottleneck, we introduce Velox, a training-free attention router for region-level context selection using frustum slab correspondences from streaming geometry. In this work, we propose a steering agent that supervises execution on the fly and steers the system back on track when drift occurs. The steering agent observes the execution context, deciding when to intervene with a low-cost fast path and how to intervene with a high-cost slow path. Experiments on information-seeking search tasks show that the steering agent provides effective supervision for both single-agent and multi-agent systems, improving their performance and efficiency.   • Abstract: Mixture-of-experts (MoE) models have become an important approach for scaling model parameters. Modern MoE architectures increasingly combine two design choices: fine-grained expert segmentation and shared experts. The former increases expressiveness by enabling more expert combinations at a fixed activation ratio, and the latter introduces experts that are active for every token. We identify an exploration dilemma between these designs: shared experts can make routing less exploratory by reducing the variability of token representations. This conflicts with the greater need for exploration induced by fine-grained expert segmentation due to the combinatorially enlarged routing space. Consequently, the additional expressiveness offered by fine-grained segmentation may not be fully realized in practice. MirrorMoE is proposed to reconcile this exploration dilemma by shrinking the exploration space through token-adaptive suppression of unnecessary experts. Our model integrates a compact, structured 3D action representation into a pretrained video diffusion transformer through a mixture-of-transformers architecture with shared base weights, modality-specific adaptation, and bidirectional visual-action attention. Randomized modality visibility lets video and 3D actions serve as either conditioning context or prediction targets across the three modes. Experiments show that unified training improves all three capabilities over single-mode training, including gains in planning quality under a vision-language model evaluation. Through unified training, our model outperforms specialized baselines on 3D hand reconstruction and action-conditioned video prediction across four datasets, while producing goal-directed manipulation videos with stronger instruction adherence and action progression than video-generation baselines. Project Page: this https URL

Title: SoftCache: Adaptive Step Size Caching for Diffusion Transformers

  • Authors: Shimon Malnick, Nadav Orenstein, Ohad Fried, Shai Avidan
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22369
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22369
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22369]] SoftCache: Adaptive Step Size Caching for Diffusion Transformers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22369)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Diffusion and flow models generate a sample over iterative steps, each a full pass through a large network, and that pass dominates generation cost. Training-free caching accelerates them by reusing activations from recent evaluations instead of recomputing them. Dynamic caching methods decide when to refresh by accumulating a cheap probe of the network input into a staleness signal and spending a fresh evaluation once it crosses a threshold. They perform hard caching: the signal is a binary switch between refresh and reuse, and every step, fresh or cached, advances by the prescribed step size. We observe that the signal carries much more than this switch. How far it sits below the threshold, its headroom, predicts the error induced by reuse. We propose SoftCache, which performs soft caching: instead of a binary switch, the headroom sets how far each cached step goes. Where the headroom is large, the sampler jumps past several scheduled nodes, and as a refresh nears, its steps shrink back toward the original step. These adaptive jumps may land between the scheduled nodes, taking the sampler off its fixed grid. SoftCache requires no training and adds no per-backbone settings: on FLUX for images and on HunyuanVideo and Wan2.1 for video, at speedups from $3.5\times$ to $6.9\times$, it is ahead of every baseline on all four fidelity metrics.

Title: Which Tokens to Merge? Diffusion Dynamics for Efficient Image Generation

  • Authors: SeungJu Cha, Ye-Chan Kim, HyunGee Kim, Sungho Koh, Dong-Jin Kim
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22370
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22370
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22370]] Which Tokens to Merge? Diffusion Dynamics for Efficient Image Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22370)
  • Keywords: protect, diffusion
  • Abstract: Token merging has emerged as an effective training-free strategy for accelerating diffusion models by reducing redundant token computation while minimizing generation degradation. A key challenge is determining which tokens can be safely merged and which should be preserved for subsequent computation. Existing method addresses this by preserving prompt-aligned foreground regions, thereby protecting semantically relevant structures. However, this text-relevance criterion overlooks background objects, local details that are not explicitly mentioned in the prompt, leading to degraded structural coherence. In this work, we propose Dynamics-aware Token Merging (DaTMe), a training-free framework that grounds token merging in the underlying diffusion dynamics. Our key insight is that token importance should reflect whether a token remains unresolved during denoising and whether it carries structural information. To this end, DaTMe constructs a dynamics- aware importance map from the Tweedie denoised estimate. The map combines a temporal signal, measuring inter-step changes in the predicted clean image, with a structural signal, capturing local high-frequency content such as boundaries and fine details. Using this map, DaTMe adaptively assigns tokens into merging roles under a given compression ratio: important tokens are preserved as anchors, while tokens that are both temporally stabilized and structurally homogeneous are selected as safe merge candidates. Experiments on PixArt-a and FLUX demonstrate that DaTMe maintains visual fidelity across the entire image, highlighting diffusion dynamics as an effective token-level merging decision in efficient generation. Our code is available at this https URL.

Title: Gen4P: Video Generators are 4D Perceivers

  • Authors: Yitong Xia, Zeren Jiang, Chuanxia Zheng
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22371
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22371
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.22371]] Gen4P: Video Generators are 4D Perceivers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22371)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: We introduce Gen4P, a unified framework for recovering 4D geometry and motion from monocular video. We show that a pretrained video generator can be repurposed into a feed-forward 4D perceiver without iterative denoising. Building on on-demand querying, Gen4P processes clean video latents in a single encoder pass and predicts the requested 4D attributes through a lightweight cross-attention decoder. The on-demand query formulation enables flexible spatial sampling, and our proposed continuous-time queries further provide temporal flexibility. To strengthen geometric knowledge, we propose a straightforward plug-in solution by incorporating a pretrained video-depth LoRA as a frozen geometry prior, improving overall performance without adding extra trainable parameters. Gen4P achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance on tasks of 3D point tracking, video depth and camera pose estimation. Qualitative results on in-the-wild videos illustrate its capability of tracking complex motion while maintaining the delicate structure. Together, these results demonstrate the potential of video generative pretraining for unified 4D perception. See this https URL for more visual results.
